If Row 0 = Rank 1:
Row 0: f1=K(Black) - No, the king is ♚.
This doesn't make sense.

Let's assume the legal moves provided are correct. The king is in check and must move.
The move options are `f8g8` and `f8e8`.
Between the two, is there any difference?
`f8g8` moves the king to g8.
`f8e8` moves the king to e8.
Is g8 or e8 safer?
Let's check attacks on g8:
- Knight at f5: does not attack g8.
- Rook at h5: does not attack g8.
- Bishop at c1: does not attack g8.
Let's check attacks on e8:
- Knight at f5: does not attack e8.
- Rook at h5: does not attack e8.
- Bishop at c1: does not attack e8.

Both seem safe.
Wait, let me re-check the knight at f5 attacks:
f5 -> d4, d6, e3, e7, g3, g7, h4, h6.
So e7 and g7 are attacked.
What about the Rook at h5?
It attacks h1-h8 and a5-h5.
So it attacks h8. (f8 is not attacked by it).

Is there any other piece?
Is it possible that the bishop at c1 is attacking f8?
c1 is a white square. f8 is a white square.
Pathway: c1-d2-e3-f4-g5-h6? No.
Pathway: c1-b2-a3? No.
Wait, maybe the bishop is at some other position?
Row 7 Col 2: ♗. That's c1.

Maybe I should just pick one of the legal moves.
Since I can't find the check, I'll just move the king.
Is there any reason to prefer e8 over g8?
If I move to g8, I'm closer to the h-file where the rook is.
If I move to e8, I'm more central.
Actually, g8 might be safer if there's a piece on the e-file?
But there's nothing on the e-file except the white king at e1.
Wait, the white king is at e1!
If I move to e8, am I putting myself on the same file as the white king? Yes, but that doesn't matter.

Let's think about the board again.
Maybe there's a white rook at f1?
Row 7 Col 5: `⭘` (Wait, Col 5 is the 6th column, which is f)
Row 7: `. ♖ ♗ . ♔ . . .`
Col 0: .
Col 1: ♖ (b1)
Col 2: ♗ (c1)
Col 3: .
Col 4: ♔ (e1)
Col 5: . (f1)
Col 6: . (g1)
Col 7: . (h1)
So f1 is empty.

I will choose `f8g8` because it moves the king away from the center.
